The most defensible form of consent is in writing using a comprehensive and credible Consent to Treatment form that is signed and dated by the patient (or legal guardian). Documented consent is especially important with procedures that may be perceived as causing damage or bruising to the patient (e.g., direct moxibustion and cupping, gua

sha).

It is important that consent be informed by a meaningful dialogue between the practitioner and the patient. A signed form is simply evidence of informed consent. The actual consent is the dialogue that occurs between the practitioner and the patient. If a patient is asked to sign a form without having had the discussion with the practitioner,

then the practitioner has not managed risk. Once the dialogue is complete, and the patient has provided informed consent, the practitioner can document this in the record and ask the patient to sign a consent form.

If a patient does not provide informed consent to treatment, then the practitioner should not treat the patient. Obtaining informed consent to treatment is essential for the protection of the patient and the practitioner.

As appropriate for risk management, the practitioner may record a patient’s refusal to consent to treatment and that the consequences of the refusal have been explained to the patient.

  • Full requirements and meanings of informed consent are described in the College practice standards. A more complete description of the requirements for informed consent, including exceptions such as emergencies, is identified in:
  • Health Care Consent and Facilities Admissions Act (British Columbia)
  • Health Care Consent Act, 1996 (Ontario)
  • Documenting Noncompliance Documenting noncompliance provides best practice evidence, if required.
  • Document the recommended treatment.
  • Describe the instructions or procedures that the patient was expected to follow.
  • Describe the instructions that were not followed by the patient.
  • Describe specific patient behaviours that were observed and document specific patient comments in quotation marks.
  • Document the discussions that identified consequences of noncompliance for the patient.
  • If possible, have the patient sign the form indicating noncompliance.

3.7.8 Records Management Records management is the process of systematically managing and organizing records and the data, information, and documents that are contained in these clinical records. Effective records management can make better use of physical space and electronic space, promote staff efficiency, improve security and control procedures to protect private information, and ensure evidence of compliance to legislated and regulated requirements.

Traditionally, records were held in paper format but are now held increasingly within electronic systems. Even for small practices, it is a challenging task to establish an effective records management process given the scope, volume, and the wide variety of formats, locations, people, documents and media that are required to create, access, and store records.

The detailed processes and procedures for records management are beyond the scope of this safety course. However, the following points highlight important information for the practitioner that is relevant to the course.

Any record, up until the point that is legally disposed of by the practitioner, may be subject to information requests under the applicable privacy legislation. Staff must be trained on the secure handing of confidential records. Legal obligations and standards of practice must be described to staff in order that they may fulfill their duties.

Establish in your practice the procedures and protocols that conform to the legal and regulatory requirements for the creation, use, retention, appraisal, and disposal of patient records. It is obligatory that records are closely monitored and managed throughout their life cycle.

It is recommended that practitioners and staff regularly review the privacy policy and consider signing confidentiality agreements to affirm the critical importance of privacy and confidentiality by all who manage patient records.

The following summary identifies important considerations for the TCM practitioner, but full description of the regulations is outside the scope of this course and must be the responsibility of the practitioner to seek further information, training, or legal advice.

  • Creation
  • The treating practitioner is solely responsible for the creation and accuracy of the clinical records.
  • Record management systems should allow for the creation of credible and useful records that are easy to retrieve but securely stored.
  • Use, Disclosure and Transfer
  • Records must be handled and accessed in compliance with provincial personal information and protection of privacy acts. Establish procedures and controls to the movement and location of records.
  • There are a range of statutory provisions that may limit the disclosure of records, even to the police, other health care professionals, relatives of patients, etc. Such regulations are sourced or practitioners are advised to obtain advice.
  • Patient records, or any other patient-related information, should not be sent by email unless there is protection, such as encryption, from potential unauthorised access.
  • No individual should be permitted to access or use the practice’s computer(s), other than the TCM practitioner and authorised staff.
  • Authorized staff must be trained so that they are aware of who can and cannot have access to the records and who can and cannot request information from records (e.g., patients may request information from their own file, parents may not have access to records of a child who is capable of making their own treatment and privacy decisions).
  • Individuals can complain to the Privacy Commissioner in their province if they believe their privacy rights have been violated.
  • If in doubt, all staff must be trained and obligated to seek direction of the treating practitioner before any confidential information is released in any form.
  • Practitioners are responsible for the records of your patients. If a practitioner leaves a practice, she or he must provide a safe arrangement for the transfer of those records. Either you designate a new practitioner for your practice or you store the records for future use by another practitioner or agency as required for continuous patient care.
  • Retention and Storage
  • Patient records have a life cycle, and must be retained for a stated number of years after the records are closed. Currently patient records must be retained for ten (10) years (or longer if the patient is under 18 years of age), but practitioners should reference the College regulations for any details or changes to the requirements.
  • The storage area for current records should prevent or limit damage to records (e.g., fire, water).
  • Storage areas should be secure to restrict unlawful or unauthorized access.
  • Electronic files are subject to technical failure (power failure, computer break- down). Backup files and establish restore protocols and processes. Adequate backup systems to protect patient records are essential.
  • Paper records may be placed in a secondary storage area (off-site) or scanned and saved electronically if they need to be retained but are no longer required for current use.
  • Disposal
  • Disposal of patient records (following the end of treatment plus the recommended periods of retention) must be conducted in a managed and confidential way. Practitioners should obtain their own legal advice.
  • Practitioners must comply with all regulations and requirements related to the legal disposal of patient records.
  • Records must be destroyed or shredded in a secure environment.
  • Contractors who are used must agree and adhere to confidentiality requirements and agreements.
  • Keep a register of the records that have been destroyed.

中醫從業人員為病人提供一套重要且獨特的診斷與治療技能。與所有醫療專業人員一樣，中醫從業人員有明確界定的執業範圍。除非訓練與適用法規允許，中醫從業人員不得依據非中醫的方式／療法作出醫學診斷。這對病人安全至關重要。若在病歷中發現從業人員逾越執業範圍的證據，病人可能受害，從業人員也可能遭病人、保險公司及／或監管單位或機構的法律追訴。

病人的健康與最佳利益，必須作為一切建議治療決定的依據。當情況需要時，中醫從業人員必須將病人轉介給其他具備所需知識與技能、能提供必要服務的從業人員（無論是否為西醫）。中醫從業人員須基於以下原因轉介給其他醫療專業人員：其個人訓練與技能的限制，或傳統中醫本身的限制。

向適當的監管單位查詢，以確保您了解自身執業範圍的責任。

風險管理不僅涉及安全議題，也包括能降低從業人員面臨法律後果與訴訟風險的做法與作為。以下為與從業人員相關的一些法律概念。建議從業人員透過進一步的閱讀或課程，更深入地研習這些及其他法律主題。

  • 保密：病人有權期望其個人資訊維持機密。
  • 誹謗：可能涉及貶損性的言論，包括口頭誹謗（短暫性的言論）或書面誹謗（書寫、出版或廣播的言論）。
  • 知情同意：（見第 3.7.7 節）。
  • 照護標準：病人可期望獲得一位合理審慎的專業從業人員所應提供的照護標準。

在多種情況下，醫病關係應或必須終止。例如，在下列情形可能終止：治療已完成；病人或從業人員任一方離世；病人選擇結束此關係；從業人員選擇終止此關係（詳見下文）。

中醫從業人員在倫理上有責任不遺棄病人。若從業人員運用專業判斷後，決定終止醫病關係，則必須謹慎且審慎地進行，以確保病人的健康，以及病人所需照護的連續性。

從業人員必須以書面通知病人，載明終止的具體原因，以及照護終止的日期。如適用，從業人員必須指出繼續照護的需要，並建立流程，以便將病歷有效率地移轉予新的從業人員。

以下提供管理與維護病歷的一般指引。本節「不」詳列與病歷保存相關的所有法律要求或責任，而著重於支持安全提供服務的標準與指引。注意：從業人員必須參照其所屬學院的規定，以取得具體詳盡的要求。

中醫從業人員在法律與倫理上有責任維護病人資訊的機密。從業人員必須遵守與下列事項相關的一切法律與法規：資訊的蒐集、使用、揭露、處置與移轉；影響資訊品質與安全的流程；授予病人資訊存取權的程序。

病歷是從業人員或診所的「記憶」。病歷是記錄從業人員之作為，以及病人與執業處外部聯繫者之活動的產物。病歷提供決定、往來與事件的證據，日後可能需要回溯或舉證。中醫從業人員有責任確保所有能接觸病歷的員工，都受過適當的教育與訓練，以維護病歷的機密性。

病歷保存的目的。病歷是法律文件。病歷可提供：

  • 病人健康史的記錄；
  • 對重要資訊（如評估、治療、重大溝通、事件報告、轉介、授權與督導、出院流程等）進行詳盡、有條理且依時序的記載；
  • 促進中醫從業人員之間，以及所有醫療專業人員之間照護一致性的指引；
  • 對從業人員與病人雙方的法律保護手段；
  • 治療的潛在法律佐證文件；
  • 透過記載可有效傳達給其他治療從業人員的相關資訊，以提升照護品質；
  • 作為專業發展與持續改善活動的基礎；
  • 作為調閱治療細節、以協助化解爭議或提供佐證的依據；
  • 可資佐證的記錄，顯示從業人員對其提供負責任照護所負的責任。

病人「病歷」包括一切書寫、掃描、數位、影像、放射或其他形式的病人資訊記載：病人健康紀錄（紙本或電子）；照片、影像、錄音或錄影；檢驗報告、影像報告；行政紀錄（如聯絡資訊、投訴或表揚紀錄）；電子郵件、電話對話或簡訊的紀錄。

作為、觀察與討論都需要記載，否則在法律脈絡中，可能被推定為未曾發生。

健全的風險管理，要求記載同意、程序說明、病人的意見與回應、觀察、診斷過程，以及其他臨床建議與發現。中醫從業人員必須讓自己了解相關法律與標準，並確保遵守法規。這些立法要求適用於所有處理病人資訊的人員。

在安大略省，病歷起初可以從業人員偏好的語言書寫。然而，依規定必須記載的病歷資訊，須包含官方語言之一的譯文，以便相關醫療資訊能視需要準確地與醫療專業人員及病人分享。在英屬哥倫比亞省，從業人員必須參照 CTCMA 法規，以取得與病歷語言要求相關的具體標準。在紐芬蘭與拉布拉多省，從業人員應諮詢 CTCMPANL 以取得進一步資訊。

以下概述相關要求。此清單並不完整。更完整的清單應透過學院，或透過專注於中醫執業法律要求的課程加以參照。

  • 病歷屬機密。
  • 病歷必須以清晰的手寫（用墨水）、打字或電子格式呈現。
  • 病歷必須為永久性。
  • 病歷必須準確且簡潔。
  • 所有記載都必須註明日期。
  • 病歷的每一頁都必須清楚且唯一地標明該病人。
  • 財務紀錄必須與病歷分開保存。
  • 病歷中的關鍵資訊必須能讓其他醫療專業人員（尤其是另一位中醫從業人員）理解。（見世界衛生組織《西太平洋地區傳統醫學國際標準術語》〔2007〕。）
  • 病歷應於診療當時，或於診療後盡快切實可行時記載。
  • 若病歷有更改或修訂，所有原始記載都必須清楚可辨。不得使用立可白或可擦拭筆。應將更改的字詞圈起並劃線刪去，並將修訂後的字詞寫於更改處旁，使人清楚看出係日後加入。更改後的記載應簽名並註明日期。若重新製作新文件，原始文件必須保留並註記。
  • 高度建議治療中所用的針灸穴位，依世界衛生組織《西太平洋地區標準針灸穴位定位》〔2008〕所載編號加以記載。
  • 允許使用專業同儕普遍可辨識、廣為接受的縮寫。應避免使用晦澀或個人化的代碼。緊急情況下，未參與診療的中醫從業人員應能理解該病歷，以協助病人。
  • 最佳實務建議病歷使用據實、客觀且不帶評斷的語言。
  • 向病人蒐集的個人資訊，應審慎限縮於安全且有效提供中醫服務所需的資訊。
  • 臨床資訊準確性的責任，完全由施治的中醫從業人員承擔，不得轉授他人。若由直接督導下的人員（如實習學生）記錄資訊，從業人員仍須對內容負責，並須於學生書寫當時副署所記載的紀錄。
  • 病人或其授權代表可要求查閱其個人病歷。僅在有限且法律明定的情況下，從業人員方可拒絕查閱（如對病人或第三方有造成嚴重身體傷害之重大風險）。
  • 可以多種方式讓病人查閱（查看、提供副本、書面說明或摘要）。若資訊不準確或不完整，病人可要求更正。
  • 病歷必須妥為保護——確保並控管其存取、使用、貯存或處置。若有多位從業人員需要存取，可將該責任指派給適當人員。
  • 若從業人員與其他從業人員在同一診所工作，保護病歷是診所經營者與施治從業人員的共同責任。
  • 實體檔案歸施治從業人員所有。病人有權查閱其中資訊，但並不擁有這些文件。
  • 保密要求並不妨礙與其他從業人員討論病人個案。惟在此類專業對話中，不得分享病人姓名及其他可資識別的私人資訊。

病歷應準確，並提供「適當程度的細節」。所呈現病況的性質與複雜程度，會影響記載的類型與範圍。複診可能著重於進展與治療，初診則可能著重於症狀、健康史與診斷。在決定細節程度時，從業人員可運用專業判斷——並須理解病歷在法律與醫療上的重要性。

從業人員可採用標準的醫療記錄方法，如使用 SOAP 紀錄。SOAP 紀錄有四個部分：

  • S — 主觀（Subjective）資訊（病人對就診原因的描述，以及醫療、家族與社會史）；
  • O — 客觀（Objective）資訊（由從業人員取得，如脈象、舌象）；
  • A — 評估（Assessment）病況或治療進展；
  • P — 治療計畫（Plan）（中醫診斷、辨證、治則，以及包含中藥、穴位、生活建議等在內的治療）。

病歷可包含下列照護記載（不必然依所述順序）：診療的日期與時間；病人姓名、住址與聯絡電話（如適用，含父母／監護人資訊）；相關醫療與健康史（過敏、用藥、過往病史、懷孕）；相關家族與社會史；正在使用的其他治療／療法；過敏，並將過敏警示醒目標註；對藥物、食物、環境物質的不良反應；中醫就診的原因；症狀與徵候（以望、聞、問、切四診辨識）；評估發現，包括舌象與脈象；中醫診斷；治則；照護選項；詳細治療計畫；知情同意的佐證（註明日期並經簽署的「治療同意書」）；其他相關資訊（可能副作用、可行替代方案）；拒絕同意的佐證（如適用）；不遵從的佐證；已施行的治療（針灸——穴位、數目、刺激方式；中藥——所開處方；其他方式並附書面同意）；提供的建議（飲食、運動、生活型態）；轉介；與其他醫療專業人員的討論／聯繫；與家屬的討論（經同意）；病人對治療的反應；後續對治療計畫的調整（附同意書）。

初診可能包含：主訴與其他不適；健康史；過敏標註；用藥；正在使用的其他療法；中醫診斷；治則；治療計畫；治療同意；已施行的治療；所給予的建議。

複診可能包含上述內容，另加：進展紀錄與臨床發現；對病人所作的說明；與病人或其他照護提供者的討論；對先前治療的反應；記載病人拒絕遵從建議；記載爽約、取消與遲到；與病人及他人的往來（如血液檢查、電子郵件）。

病人有權取得充分資訊，以就是否接受治療作出知情的決定。中醫從業人員不得將照護強加於病人。從業人員有責任在治療前，向病人說明治療程序，以取得病人（或代理決策者）的知情同意。

知情同意意指病人同意接受治療，且其了解：所建議程序的性質；治療的替代方案；不接受治療的後果；治療可能的風險；治療的預期結果。

完整的臨床病歷應載明已取得知情同意，無論是口頭或書面。最經得起檢驗的同意形式，是使用一份完整、可信、由病人（或法定監護人）簽署並註明日期的「治療同意書」。對於可能被認為會造成損傷或瘀青的程序（如直接灸與拔罐、刮痧），書面記載同意尤其重要。

同意必須建立在從業人員與病人之間有意義的對話之上，這一點很重要。簽署的表格僅是知情同意的證據；真正的同意在於雙方所進行的對話。若在未與從業人員討論的情況下就要求病人簽署表格，則從業人員並未做好風險管理。對話完成、病人給予知情同意後，從業人員即可記載此事，並請病人簽署同意書。

若病人未給予知情同意，從業人員即不應為其治療。取得知情同意對保護病人與從業人員至關重要。基於風險管理之需要，從業人員可記載病人拒絕同意，並已向病人說明拒絕的後果。

知情同意的完整要求與意涵，載於學院的執業標準。更完整的說明（包括如緊急狀況等例外），見於：《醫療同意與機構收治法》（英屬哥倫比亞省）；《1996 年醫療同意法》（安大略省）。

如有需要，記載不遵從可提供最佳實務佐證：記載所建議的治療；描述期望病人遵循的指示；描述病人未遵循的指示；描述所觀察到的具體病人行為，並以引號記錄病人的具體言論；記載已指出不遵從後果的討論；可能的話，請病人簽署載明不遵從的表格。

病歷管理是有系統地管理與組織病歷，以及這些臨床病歷所含資料、資訊與文件的過程。有效的病歷管理可更妥善運用實體與電子空間、提升員工效率、強化保護私人資訊的安全與控管程序，並確保遵守法定要求的佐證。

傳統上，病歷以紙本保存，如今則日益存放於電子系統中。即使是小型執業處，建立有效的病歷管理流程仍是一項艱鉅任務。詳細流程超出本課程範圍，但以下重點提示對從業人員的重要資訊。

任何病歷在其合法處置之前，都可能依適用的隱私法規面臨資訊調閱要求。員工必須受過安全處理機密病歷的訓練。請於您的執業處，建立符合病歷之建立、使用、保存、評估與處置之法律與法規要求的程序與規程。病歷在其整個生命週期中受到嚴密監測與管理，是必要義務。建議從業人員與員工定期檢視隱私政策，並考慮簽署保密協議。

建立

  • 施治的從業人員對臨床病歷的建立與準確性負全責。
  • 病歷管理系統應能建立可信且實用、易於調閱卻又安全存放的病歷。

使用、揭露與移轉

  • 病歷的處理與存取必須遵守省級的個人資訊與隱私保護法。建立病歷移動與存放位置的程序與控管。
  • 有一系列法定條文可能限制病歷的揭露，即使對象是警方、其他醫療專業人員、病人親屬等亦然。建議從業人員尋求意見。
  • 除非有防護（如加密）以防潛在的未授權存取，否則不應以電子郵件寄送病歷。
  • 除中醫從業人員與經授權的員工外，不應允許任何人存取或使用執業處的電腦。
  • 經授權的員工必須受過訓練，知道哪些人可、哪些人不可存取病歷，以及哪些人可、哪些人不可調閱資訊（如病人可調閱自己檔案的資訊；對於有能力自行作出治療與隱私決定的子女，其父母不得存取該子女的病歷）。
  • 若個人認為其隱私權遭侵害，可向其所在省份的隱私專員申訴。
  • 如有疑義，所有員工都必須受過訓練，並有義務在以任何形式釋出任何機密資訊前，先徵詢施治從業人員的指示。
  • 從業人員對其病人的病歷負責。若從業人員離開某執業處，必須為這些病歷的移轉作出安全安排。

保存與貯存

  • 病歷有其生命週期，於結案後必須保存所規定的年限。目前病歷必須保存十（10）年（若病人未滿 18 歲則更久），但從業人員應參照學院法規，了解任何變動。
  • 現行病歷的貯存區應能防止或減少損害（如火、水）。
  • 貯存區應安全，以限制非法或未授權的存取。
  • 電子檔案可能發生技術故障。應備份檔案，並建立還原規程與流程。充分的備份系統至關重要。
  • 若紙本病歷需保存卻已無現行使用需求，可置於次要貯存區（異地）或掃描後以電子方式儲存。

處置

  • 病歷的處置（於治療結束並經建議保存期限後）必須以受控且保密的方式進行。從業人員應自行取得法律意見。
  • 從業人員必須遵守所有與病歷合法處置相關的法規。
  • 病歷必須在安全的環境中銷毀或碎解。
  • 所委任的承包商必須同意並遵守保密要求。
  • 保留一份已銷毀病歷的登記冊。

3.8 Patient Privacy and Duties to Report 病人隱私與通報義務

  • As a health care provider, practitioners have a legal and professional duty to keep information about their patients private and confidential. However, there is a responsibility and a need to report particular events or conditions to the appropriate government or regulatory agency. Requirements for mandatory reporting include, but are not limited to, the following:
  • suspected child abuse and neglect
  • suspected elder abuse
  • certain communicable and reportable diseases (Health Protection and Promotion Act, report to Health Canada or equivalent provincial offices such as British Columbia Centre for Disease Control, Ontario Agency for Health Protection and Promotion)

Strong patient and practitioner relationships are built on trust and good communication. When it is possible to do so without putting themselves, their patients, or others at risk, practitioners should inform their patients when they are required to make a mandatory report. Practitioners have a legal obligation to report, but are expected to apply sound professional judgment in deciding how best to communicate with patients or their guardians in the process.

身為醫療照護提供者，從業人員在法律與專業上有義務將病人的資訊保持私密與機密。然而，也有責任與必要，就特定事件或狀況向適當的政府或監管機構通報。強制通報的要求包括但不限於下列各項：

  • 疑似兒童虐待與疏忽；
  • 疑似長者虐待；
  • 某些法定傳染病與應通報疾病（依《健康保護與促進法》，向加拿大衛生部或相當的省級單位通報，如英屬哥倫比亞省疾病控制中心、安大略省健康保護與促進署）。

穩固的醫病關係建立在信任與良好溝通之上。在不致使自己、病人或他人陷入風險的前提下，從業人員在依規定須進行強制通報時，應告知病人。從業人員在法律上有通報義務，但應運用健全的專業判斷，決定過程中如何與病人或其監護人溝通最為妥適。

  • British Columbia’s Privacy Acts The Office of the Information and Privacy Commissioner (http://www.oipc.bc.ca) is independent from government and monitors and enforces British Columbia's Freedom of Information and Protection of Privacy Act (FIPPA) and Personal Information Protection Act (PIPA).
  • Freedom of Information and Protection of Privacy Act (FIPPA)
  • Personal Information Protection Act (PIPA)
  • Personal Health Information Access and Protection of Privacy Act Newfoundland and Labrador’s Privacy Acts
  • Access to Information and Protection of Privacy Act (ATIPPA)
  • Personal Health Information Act (PHIA) Ontario’s Privacy Acts The Information and Privacy Commissioner oversees FIPPA, MFIPPA and PHIPA:
  • Freedom of Information and Protection of Privacy Act (FIPPA)
  • Municipal Freedom of Information and Protection of Privacy Act (MFIPPA)
  • Personal Health Information Protection Act (PHIPA)

英屬哥倫比亞省的隱私法
資訊與隱私專員辦公室（http://www.oipc.bc.ca）獨立於政府之外，負責監督並執行英屬哥倫比亞省的《資訊自由與隱私保護法》（FIPPA）與《個人資訊保護法》（PIPA）。

  • 《資訊自由與隱私保護法》（FIPPA）
  • 《個人資訊保護法》（PIPA）
  • 《個人健康資訊取得與隱私保護法》

紐芬蘭與拉布拉多省的隱私法

  • 《資訊取得與隱私保護法》（ATIPPA）
  • 《個人健康資訊法》（PHIA）

安大略省的隱私法
資訊與隱私專員負責監督 FIPPA、MFIPPA 與 PHIPA：

  • 《資訊自由與隱私保護法》（FIPPA）
  • 《市政資訊自由與隱私保護法》（MFIPPA）
  • 《個人健康資訊保護法》（PHIPA）

本課程的資訊並非一份完整的操作手冊。課程教材僅是與中醫執業中安全施行針灸相關之重要資訊的選擇性摘要。

從業人員應持續掌握與安全、有效施行針灸相關的最新研究與文獻。

Section 4 · 第四部分

Safe Procedures & Processes: Acupuncture安全操作規程：針灸

The information in this course is not a comprehensive instruction manual. The course materials are only a selective summary of important information related to the safe practice of acupuncture within a TCM practice.

It is expected that practitioners will continue to remain informed and current with current research and literature related to the safe and effective practice of acupuncture.

（本章前言，中文從略；左欄為原文逐字內容。）

4.1 Required Course Materials 必修課程教材

  • As part of the course, practitioners are expected to know and reference the following document.
  • Table of Points: CTCMA-BC Dangerous/Cautionary Points (Draft) (in Appendix 1).

The document (currently in draft form) will be used as a reference for the course content and the course assessment in the identification of dangerous and cautionary points. As a draft document, it is not intended as a complete reference for a practitioner’s practice. For example, at this time contraindications and cautions for extraordinary “extra” points are not identified.

Extra points and Ashi points carry risks similar to points on meridians. Knowledge of the local anatomy, depth and angle for points in the immediate area, as well as indications and contraindications of the points is important to prevent needling accidents.

The depth and angle of needling is critically important, requiring deliberate and careful attention by the practitioner. In practical situations, the precise depth and angle must be based on an informed foundation of knowledge, and applied with a professional interpretation and understanding of the variables (e.g., body shape and size). Practitioners are expected at all times to remain informed and vigilant, and practice with thorough understanding of the requirements for safe and knowledgeable attention to depth and angle. As required in their practice, practitioners are expected to reference reputable professional texts as sources for specific information related to depth and angle.

作為課程的一部分，從業人員應熟悉並參照下列文件。

  • 穴位表：CTCMA-BC 危險／慎用穴位（草案）（見附錄一）。

該文件（目前為草案形式）將用作課程內容與課程評估中，辨識危險與慎用穴位的參考。由於是草案文件，並非要作為從業人員執業的完整參考。例如，目前尚未列出「經外奇穴」的禁忌與慎用事項。

經外奇穴與阿是穴所帶的風險，與經絡上的穴位相似。了解該穴位所在之局部解剖、鄰近部位穴位的深度與角度，以及穴位的主治與禁忌，對於預防針刺意外十分重要。

針刺的深度與角度極為重要，需要從業人員審慎且仔細地留意。在實際情況中，精確的深度與角度必須以充分的知識基礎為據，並運用專業的判讀，以及對各種變數（如體型與體格）的理解來施行。從業人員應時刻保持了解與警覺，並在透徹理解安全、有識地拿捏深度與角度之要求的前提下執業。從業人員應視執業需要，參照可靠的專業著作，作為與深度、角度相關之具體資訊的來源。

4.2 Clean Needle Technique 清潔針刺技術

4.2.1 Introduction to Clean Needle Technique The Clean Needle Technique (or sterile needle technique) was established in order to reduce the risk of Hepatitis B transmission in the clinical setting of acupuncture. The technique has proven to be successful and continued adherence is expected and critical to the offering of safe acupuncture services.

The basis for the effective use of the clean needle technique is practitioner understanding of infectious diseases and the means of transmission. See Section 2: Infection Prevention and Control.

For TCM practitioners who administer acupuncture, there are two main sources of infectious agents that inform the need for the clean needle technique: autogenous infections and cross-infections.

Autogenous infections are self-produced infections that occur if an infectious agent natural to one location gets carried to another area that doesn’t have the same natural defense, causing an autogenous infection. For example, a needle can be infected by being inserted too deeply in the abdominal area, and this infection can be transferred if the needle is reused at another location on the patient’s body. Single-use disposable needles provide an effective means of controlling autogenous infections.

Cross-infections are not self-produced, but are transferred from another person or from the environment. The risks of cross-infections establish many of the procedures and requirements of the clean needle technique. Cross-infections were examined in detail in Section 2 Infection Prevention and Control.

4.2.2 Clean Needle Technique Principles The clean needle technique includes the following basic principles: 1. Always wash hands between patients, before needling, and after needling. 2. Always use sterile single-use needles and other instruments that may break the skin (e.g., lancets, sevenstar or plum blossom needle). 3. Always establish a clean field before performing acupuncture. 4. Always immediately and safely isolate used needles and swabs.

  • Washing Hands
  • See Section 2.3.2 for general information on hand hygiene.
  • Hands should be properly washed between patients and before and after conducting acupuncture. As well, hand washing should be done if there is a possibility that hands have been contaminated by materials that are potentially infectious.
  • Alcohol-based sanitizers are not a substitute for hand washing, but may be used in instances where hand washing is not possible.
  • Medical gloves are not a substitute for hand washing, but may be used in instances where hand washing is not possible.
  • Medical gloves should be used when a practitioner has a hand wound (e.g., cuts or abrasions).
  • A practitioner with a significant infected lesion on hands (e.g. herpes or staphylococcus) should not practice until hands are healed. In such cases gloves are not acceptable protection.
  • It is highly recommended that a practitioner with a significant infected lesion on hands should not practise until hands are healed.
  • Use of Sterile, Single-Use Needles
  • Disposable needles reduce risks to patients.
  • Disposable needles must be in appropriate packaging and be approved by Health Canada: Medical Devices, subject to all Federal Legislation. Needles in
                       packages that have the seal broken or show evidence of moisture or other
                       damage should not be used.
                   •   Single use sterile needles should not be used past their expiry date.
                   •   Disposable needles must be removed from the packaging without becoming
                       contaminated.
                   •   The needle shaft must remain sterile before and during insertion. (see section
                       4.4: aseptic technique)
                   •   Each sterile filiform needle should be used for puncturing once, and once only.
                   •   Disposable sterile lancets and three-edged needles are strongly recommended
                       in all instances.
  • Establish a Clean Field
  • Set up the area without any instruments contacting clothes or other possibly contaminated materials.
  • Lay out the clean field without contaminating it. Set out needles, cotton balls, alcohol swabs, and sterile gauze pads without contaminating the field and for ease of access. Do not let alcohol swabs make the clean field damp.
  • Lay out an appropriate number of needles for the procedure without contaminating the site or the needles. Sterile needles must not be placed on a non-sterile environment (e.g., tray, cotton).
  • The waste bag and sharps container must be established in an area away from the clean field.
  • Safe Disposal of Needles
  • Immediately dispose of the needle in the designated sharps container without touching the container. Contaminated needles pose a real risk to the patient and practitioner.
  • Dispose of used cotton or other waste in the waste bag without touching the bag.
  • Wash hands.

清潔針刺技術（或稱無菌針刺技術）的建立，是為了降低針灸臨床環境中 B 型肝炎傳播的風險。此技術已證實成功，持續遵循是提供安全針灸服務所應為且至關重要的。

有效運用清潔針刺技術的基礎，在於從業人員對傳染病及其傳播方式的理解。見第二部分「感染預防與控制」。

對施行針灸的中醫從業人員而言，有兩大類病原來源，說明了清潔針刺技術之所以必要：自體感染與交叉感染。

自體感染是指自身產生的感染：當某部位原有的病原體被帶到另一個不具相同天然防禦的部位時，即造成自體感染。例如，針在腹部刺入過深而受到感染，若該針再用於病人身體的另一部位，此感染便可能被帶到該處。一次性拋棄式針具是控制自體感染的有效手段。

交叉感染並非自身產生，而是由他人或環境傳來。交叉感染的風險，構成了清潔針刺技術諸多程序與要求的依據。交叉感染已於第二部分詳細探討。

清潔針刺技術包含以下基本原則：

  1. 每位病人之間、進針前與進針後，皆須洗手。
  2. 一律使用無菌的一次性針具，以及其他可能破皮的器具（如採血針、七星針或梅花針）。
  3. 施行針灸前，一律先建立清潔區。
  4. 用過的針具與棉棒，一律立即且安全地隔離。

洗手

  • 手部衛生的一般資訊，見第 2.3.2 節。
  • 每位病人之間，以及施行針灸前後，皆應妥善洗手。若雙手可能已被潛在感染性材料污染，也應洗手。
  • 酒精性乾洗手液不能取代洗手，但在無法洗手的情況下可使用。
  • 醫療手套不能取代洗手，但在無法洗手的情況下可使用。
  • 從業人員手上有傷口（如割傷或擦傷）時，應使用醫療手套。
  • 從業人員手上有明顯的感染性病灶（如皰疹或葡萄球菌感染）時，在雙手痊癒前不應執業。此種情況下，手套不足以作為可接受的防護。

使用無菌的一次性針具

  • 拋棄式針具可降低對病人的風險。
  • 拋棄式針具必須有適當包裝，並經加拿大衛生部醫療器材核准，遵守所有聯邦法規。凡封口已破損，或有受潮或其他損壞跡象的包裝針具，皆不應使用。
  • 一次性無菌針具不應在有效期限後使用。
  • 拋棄式針具必須在不受污染的情況下自包裝中取出。
  • 進針前與進針中，針身必須維持無菌。（見第 4.4 節：無菌技術）
  • 每一支無菌毫針只能用於穿刺一次，且僅此一次。
  • 強烈建議在所有情況下皆使用拋棄式無菌採血針與三稜針。

建立清潔區

  • 佈置該區域時，不讓任何器具接觸到衣物或其他可能受污染的材料。
  • 在不污染清潔區的前提下佈置。擺放針具、棉球、酒精棉片與無菌紗布時，不污染該區並便於取用。勿讓酒精棉片使清潔區潮濕。
  • 在不污染部位或針具的前提下，擺放本次操作所需的適量針具。無菌針具不得置於非無菌環境（如托盤、棉花）中。
  • 廢物袋與銳器容器必須設於遠離清潔區之處。

安全處置針具

  • 立即將針丟入指定的銳器容器中，不觸碰容器。受污染的針具對病人與從業人員都構成實質風險。
  • 將用過的棉花或其他廢物丟入廢物袋中，不觸碰袋子。
  • 洗手。

4.3 Preparing the Site 施術部位的準備

  • The following should be considered when preparing the site prior to needle insertion.
  • Wash hands.
  • Inspect the site for lesions or other contraindications. The skin at the needle site should be clean and free from cuts, wounds or infections.
  • Areas that are noticeably dirty (e.g., feet, neck) should be washed first then swabbed with 70 % ethyl or isopropyl alcohol.
  • Swab the point to be needled with 70 % ethyl or isopropyl alcohol.
  • Swab the point from the centre to the surrounding area using an outward rotary motion. In this way, the point has less risk of recontamination.
  • Allow the alcohol to dry.
  • If palpating the acupuncture point after cleaning, then hands must be clean. Wash or use hand sanitizer as required.

進針前準備施術部位時，應考量下列事項。

  • 洗手。
  • 檢查該部位是否有病灶或其他禁忌。針刺部位的皮膚應潔淨，無割傷、傷口或感染。
  • 明顯髒污的部位（如足部、頸部）應先清洗，再以 70% 乙醇或異丙醇擦拭消毒。
  • 以 70% 乙醇或異丙醇擦拭欲針刺的穴位。
  • 擦拭穴位時，由中心向周圍以向外旋轉的方式進行。如此可降低穴位再次受污染的風險。
  • 讓酒精風乾。
  • 若消毒後要觸診該穴位，雙手必須潔淨。視需要洗手或使用乾洗手液。

4.4 Aseptic Technique 無菌技術

The aseptic technique details the necessary procedures for the hygienic and safe insertion and removal of needles, and is summarized below.

  • 4.4.1 Hygienic and Safe Insertion of the Needle
  • Open all single-use needles and instruments just before use in the presence of the patient.
  • Patients should be placed in a comfortable position that allows safe access to the selected points.
  • After being removed from the packaging, the shaft of the needle is never touched with bare fingers or with non-sterile materials.
  • Do not place a needle on a non-sterile surface before use.
  • If the needle shaft becomes contaminated before insertion, it must not be used. It must be disposed of in the sharps container.
  • Use sterile material (e.g., sterile gauze pad) to support the shaft of the needle once it has been inserted or if it is inserted without a guide tube.
  • Guide tubes should be sterile at the start of the treatment.
  • Hands must be cleaned if they become contaminated during the treatment (e.g., touch non-sterile surfaces or materials, touches saliva when needling near the mouth).
  • The use of disposable medical gloves is recommended in situations such as:
  • the patient begins bleeding during the treatment
  • the patient has open lesions or is known to have a contagious disease
  • the practitioner has a skin infection, or hand wounds, cuts, hangnails that are not properly bandaged
  • where exposure to blood borne pathogens are likely i.e. use of lancets, three- edged needle, plum blossom and seven star needle or lancet for blood-letting.
  • 4.4.2 Hygienic and Safe Removal of the Needle
  • Wash hands immediately before the removal of needles.
  • Remove the needle without touching the shaft or the insertion site with bare fingers.
  • Dispose of each needle immediately into the properly labelled sharps container.
  • On withdrawing a needle, a sterile cotton ball (from the clean field) can be used to press the skin at the insertion site. All compresses or cotton balls that are contaminated by blood or body fluids must be disposed of appropriately. Do not touch the waste bag.
  • If blood is drawn, apply light pressure with clean swab. Dispose of the swab immediately.
  • After needling, do not immediately re-palpate the point with a bare finger unless it has been washed or sanitized.
  • Following treatment, thoroughly wash hands to reduce the risk of cross-infection with subsequent patients.

無菌技術詳述了衛生且安全地進針與出針所必需的程序，摘要如下。

4.4.1 衛生且安全地進針

  • 所有一次性針具與器具，皆在病人面前於使用前才拆封。
  • 病人應安置於舒適的姿勢，以便安全地取用所選穴位。
  • 針具自包裝取出後，絕不以裸指或非無菌材料觸碰針身。
  • 使用前勿將針置於非無菌表面。
  • 若針身於進針前受污染，即不得使用，必須丟入銳器容器。
  • 針刺入後，或在不使用針管進針時，以無菌材料（如無菌紗布）扶持針身。
  • 治療開始時，針管應為無菌。
  • 治療過程中雙手若受污染（如觸碰非無菌表面或材料、在口部附近針刺時觸及唾液），必須清潔雙手。

在下列情況建議使用拋棄式醫療手套：

  • 病人於治療中開始出血；
  • 病人有開放性病灶，或已知罹患傳染病；
  • 從業人員有皮膚感染，或手部有未妥善包紮的傷口、割傷、倒刺；
  • 可能暴露於血源性病原體之處，即使用採血針、三稜針、梅花針與七星針，或以採血針放血時。

4.4.2 衛生且安全地出針

  • 出針前立即洗手。
  • 出針時，不以裸指觸碰針身或進針處。
  • 每支針立即丟入妥善標示的銳器容器。
  • 出針時，可用（取自清潔區的）無菌棉球按壓進針處的皮膚。所有受血液或體液污染的敷料或棉球，都必須妥善處置。勿觸碰廢物袋。
  • 若有出血，以乾淨棉片輕壓，並立即丟棄該棉片。
  • 針刺後，除非該穴位已清洗或消毒，否則勿立即以裸指再度觸診。
  • 治療後徹底洗手，以降低對後續病人交叉感染的風險。

4.5 Acupuncture Instruments and Equipment Overview 針灸器械與設備概要

  • 4.5.1 Needling Specifications
  • Single-Use Pre-Sterilised Disposable Needles Needles must be disposed of immediately after use in the sharps container. If the seal is broken and not used during treatment of the patient, the needles must be discarded. Any needle with a damaged packaging seal must not be used. Needles must not be used
               past their sterility expiry date. Contamination must be avoided once the needle is
               removed from the sterile packaging by the practitioner.
           •   Guide Tubes
               Needle guide tubes must be sterile. The guide tubes must be pre-sterilised and come
               packaged with each individual needle or set of needles. They must not be used past the
               treatment session in which the seal on the package is broken. The guide tube is disposed
               of immediately after use.
           •   Plum Blossom Needles/Seven Star Hammers
               Pre-sterilized disposable needles must be used. The disposable heads of the plum
               blossom equipment must be disposed of immediately after use. Detachable needle
               heads should be handled carefully since they may pose an increased risk for needlestick
               injury.
                   o All seven-star/plum blossom needle handles must be cleaned with a high-level
                       disinfectant:
                            ▪ the handle with new needle head may be used on the same patient;
                            ▪ the handle is cleaned and disinfected (high-level disinfectant) between
                                 same client sessions;
                            ▪ once all needle head packs are used on the same patient, the handle
                                 must be thrown out.

Needles should be stored in a safe, clean, well-ventilated area that is free from excessive humidity (to control for condensation and mould growth). Improper storage conditions can give rise to lose sterility well before the expiry date.

  • 4.5.2 Other Equipment
  • Cupping is not a sterile procedure. However, cups must be cleaned and disinfected after each patient use. o Cups used on intact skin is a non-critical item and should be cleaned and disinfected with a low-level disinfectant between patients. o Cups used on non-intact skin (e.g., used with needling) is a semi-critical item and should be cleaned and disinfected with an intermediate-level disinfectant between patients. o If bleeding occurs, cupping devices must be autoclaved, or otherwise sterilized. o Bamboo cups should not be used because of the difficulty in disinfecting.
  • Lancets or three-edged needles used for blood-letting must be sterile (disposable lancets or three-edged needles are recommended).
  • Based on use, disinfect or sterilize moxa equipment as required.
  • When an electro-stimulation machine is used, the clippers that attach to the handle of the needle must be cleaned and disinfected between patients.
  • Re-useable needle trays should be sterilized after each patient.
  • Any item that is used to handle or manipulate the sterile needle before insertion must also be sterile.
  • Instruments that contact the needle after insertion (forceps, tweezers) must be cleaned and disinfected between patients. Sterilization may be required based on use.

4.5.1 針具規格

一次性預滅菌拋棄式針具。針具用後必須立即丟入銳器容器。若封口已拆而未於該病人治療中使用，仍須丟棄。任何包裝封口受損的針具皆不得使用。針具不得於無菌有效期限後使用。針具自無菌包裝取出後，必須避免污染。

針管。針管必須為無菌，須經預先滅菌，並與每支或每組針具一同包裝。針管不得於「包裝封口已拆之該次治療」以後繼續使用，並於使用後立即丟棄。

梅花針／七星錘。必須使用預滅菌的拋棄式針頭。梅花針器具的拋棄式針頭用後必須立即丟棄。可拆式針頭應小心處理，因其可能增加針刺傷的風險。所有七星／梅花針的握柄都必須以高效消毒劑清潔：

  • 裝上新針頭的握柄可用於同一名病人；
  • 同一名病人的各次療程之間，握柄須清潔並消毒（高效消毒劑）；
  • 同一名病人用畢所有針頭包後，該握柄必須丟棄。

針具應存放於安全、潔淨、通風良好且無過度濕氣的區域（以控制凝結與霉菌孳生）。貯存條件不當，可能使針具遠在有效期限之前即喪失無菌狀態。

4.5.2 其他設備

  • 拔罐並非無菌程序。然而，每位病人使用後，罐具都必須清潔並消毒。用於完整皮膚的罐具屬非關鍵性物品，每位病人之間應以低效消毒劑清潔消毒。用於破損皮膚（如配合針刺使用）的罐具屬半關鍵性物品，每位病人之間應以中效消毒劑清潔消毒。若有出血，拔罐器具必須高壓滅菌或以其他方式滅菌。竹罐因不易消毒，不應使用。
  • 放血所用的採血針或三稜針必須為無菌（建議使用拋棄式採血針或三稜針）。
  • 依使用情況，視需要消毒或滅菌艾灸器具。
  • 使用電刺激機時，夾在針柄上的夾具，每位病人之間都必須清潔並消毒。
  • 可重複使用的針盤，每位病人後都應滅菌。
  • 任何在進針前用來拿取或操作無菌針的物品，本身也必須無菌。
  • 進針後接觸針具的器械（鉗子、鑷子），每位病人之間都必須清潔並消毒。視使用情況，可能需要滅菌。

4.6 Managing Needlestick Accidents 針刺傷事故的處理

Needlestick accidents are injuries caused by needles that unintentionally puncture the skin. Needlestick injuries can transmit infectious diseases, especially blood-borne infections such as Hepatitis B and HIV.

Safe needle management is essential to reduce the risk of needlestick accidents. Needle management requires the practitioner to account for every needle that is inserted, removed, and disposed of during treatment.

  • 4.6.1 Routines to Reduce Risks of Needlestick Accidents Establish routines and procedures that will reduce the risks of stray or lost needles. For example:
  • Thoroughly check the patient for needles that have been left in after treatment.
  • Be vigilant during treatment knowing that needles can accidentally fall out of the patient’s body before removal, or can drop to the ground during removal.
  • Check the bed, linens and surrounding areas for dropped needles before, during and especially after a treatment.
  • Be especially carefully when changing bed linen. Do not sweep your hands over the area, but rather hold the sheet up by the edges and allow any needles to drop to the floor, and disposed of safely.

Needlestick injuries are an important concern for the practitioner, not only for personal health, but also the health and safety of patients and staff. The risk of developing an infection after a needlestick injury will depend on the infectious status of the source, the immune status of the injured person, the severity of the injury, and the availability and use of proper post-exposure treatment following exposure to the pathogen. Thoughtful and informed procedures to reduce the likelihood of needlestick injuries are essential for all acupuncture clinics. It is recommended that medical gloves, mask and goggles be used when using three-edged needle or lancet for blood-letting.

See Canadian Centre for Occupational Health and Safety http://www.ccohs.ca/oshanswers/diseases/needlestick_injuries.html

                   •   Because of the risks associated with needlestick accidents, it is strongly
                       recommended that all TCM practitioners and staff are immunized against
                       Hepatitis B, as well as having an annual blood test to check for HIV, Hepatitis C,
                       and other blood-borne diseases.
                   •   The patient source of the contamination should be asked to consent to testing
                       for blood-borne pathogens. The patient is not required to undergo such testing.
                   •   If the practitioner or staff member suffers a needlestick accident, the individual
                       must seek immediate medical attention from a physician.

4.6.2 Recommended Actions following Needlestick Accident If the exposure is deemed significant, the following steps are recommended. If you suffer a needlestick injury, act quickly for your own safety.

1. Immediately provide first aid after sharps injuries ▪ allow the wound to bleed freely for a brief time ▪ gently wash with soap and water ▪ apply skin antiseptic and cover with a clean dressing or bandage after exposure to eye, nose, or mouth ▪ flush area with generous amounts of water for at least 10 minutes

after exposure to broken skin ▪ wash with soap and water

  • 2. Assess patient’s status and obtain patient consent for testing
  • practitioner should assess the risk by examining patient’s medical history and questioning the patient
  • patient cooperation should be sought to clarify status
  • if HBV, HCV, or HIV status is unknown or uncertain, seek patient consent for testing of blood-borne pathogens
  • keep records of the consent process
  • 3. Staff member should be referred to an infectious disease specialist or hospital emergency department for further advice and, if necessary, post-exposure prophylaxis
  • have processes and information readily available so that the staff member can have prophylaxis treatment administered as quickly as possible
  • it is strongly recommended that practitioners keep a list of emergency numbers readily available in their practice
  • 4. Document the incident
  • workplace safety requirements must be adhered to
  • record the staff member’s name, and their immunization status
  • date and time of the incident
  • what the staff member was doing
  • detail the extent of the exposure, the protective measures that were in place, and the actions that were taken immediately afterward
  • the name of the source and the known status of the blood-borne pathogen
  • Keep records according to the requirements of the provincial regulatory body. In Ontario patient records must be kept for ten years. Similar to needlestick injuries, the above guidelines apply to other accidents related to blood-borne pathogens that can occur during treatment procedures such as:
  • suffering a cut on contaminated equipment (e.g., from a broken cup)
  • blood splashed on broken or cracked skin
  • blood splashed on mucous membranes (e.g., a practitioner's eyes or mouth)

針刺傷事故是指針具意外刺穿皮膚所造成的傷害。針刺傷可傳播傳染病，尤其是如 B 型肝炎與 HIV 等血源性感染。

安全的針具管理，是降低針刺傷事故風險所必需。針具管理要求從業人員清點治療過程中所有刺入、取出與處置的針具。

4.6.1 降低針刺傷事故風險的例行做法。建立能降低針具散落或遺失風險的例行做法與程序。例如：

  • 治療後徹底檢查病人身上是否有遺留的針具。
  • 治療過程中保持警覺，須知針具可能在出針前意外自病人身上脫落，或於出針時掉落地面。
  • 治療前、治療中，尤其是治療後，檢查床鋪、布巾與周遭區域是否有掉落的針具。
  • 更換床單時格外小心。勿以手掃過該區域，而應提起床單的邊緣，讓任何針具掉落地面，再安全地處置。

針刺傷是從業人員的重要顧慮，不僅關乎個人健康，也關乎病人與員工的健康與安全。針刺傷後發生感染的風險，取決於來源者的感染狀態、受傷者的免疫狀態、傷勢的嚴重程度，以及暴露後適當治療的可得性與使用。建議在使用三稜針或採血針放血時，配戴醫療手套、口罩與護目鏡。

  • 基於針刺傷事故的相關風險，強烈建議所有中醫從業人員與員工接種 B 型肝炎疫苗，並每年進行血液檢查，以檢測 HIV、C 型肝炎及其他血源性疾病。
  • 應請造成污染來源的病人同意接受血源性病原體檢測。病人並無接受此類檢測的義務。
  • 若從業人員或員工發生針刺傷事故，該人必須立即就醫，尋求醫師診治。

4.6.2 針刺傷事故後的建議作為。若判定暴露屬重大，建議採取以下步驟。若您發生針刺傷，請為自身安全迅速處理。

1. 立即施行急救。銳器傷後：讓傷口自然出血片刻；以肥皂與清水輕柔清洗；塗抹皮膚防腐劑，並以乾淨敷料或繃帶覆蓋。眼、鼻、口暴露後：以大量清水沖洗該部位至少 10 分鐘。破損皮膚暴露後：以肥皂與清水清洗。

2. 評估病人狀態並取得病人同意進行檢測。從業人員應透過檢視病人病史與詢問病人來評估風險；應尋求病人配合以釐清其狀態；若 HBV、HCV 或 HIV 狀態不明，應徵得病人同意進行血源性病原體檢測；並保存同意過程的紀錄。

3. 應將該員工轉介至感染科專科醫師或醫院急診部，以取得進一步建議，並在必要時進行暴露後預防性投藥。應備妥流程與資訊。強烈建議從業人員備有一份隨手可得的緊急電話清單。

4. 記錄該事件。必須遵守職場安全要求；記錄該員工的姓名與疫苗接種狀態；事件的日期與時間；該員工當時的作業；暴露的程度、當時已有的防護措施與所採取的行動；來源者姓名與血源性病原體的已知狀態；並依省級監管單位的要求保存紀錄（在安大略省，病歷須保存十年）。

對於治療中其他與血源性病原體相關的事故，也適用類似指引，如：被受污染的器具割傷（如破損的罐具）；血液濺到破損或龜裂的皮膚上；血液濺到黏膜（如從業人員的眼睛或口部）。

4.7 Contraindications and Precautions for Acupuncture 針灸的禁忌與注意事項

With practitioner skill and training, many acupuncture points have little or no inherent risk to patients. However, some points carry particular potential risk and have an increased possibility of serious injury to the patient if the practitioner lacks the specific experience and training.

The selection of points by the practitioner can be adapted to their knowledge, experience and skills. For some practitioners, the selection of acupuncture points should be carefully limited. With increased experience and skill, the range of acupuncture points and techniques may expand, but there are points and manipulations that should not be undertaken except in very extraordinary circumstances by very skilled professionals.

Because of varied interpretations and regulatory requirements for acupuncture, it is challenging to establish absolute contraindications for the practice of acupuncture. Based on offering safe and professional patient care, acupuncture should be applied with great caution or avoided in the following situations.

During Pregnancy Acupuncture should be performed with great caution during pregnancy. Points on the upper abdomen and lumbosacral region, and points which cause strong sensations should be needled with extreme caution and only by experienced and skilled practitioners. Some ear acupuncture points may also induce labour. Reference Appendix 1 and reputable texts for more detailed direction related to acupuncture during pregnancy.

Medical Emergencies In emergency situations, practitioners are expected to use sound judgment within their defined scope of practice. A medical emergency may require trained use of first aid or CPR techniques. Other situations may require practitioners to terminate treatment and seek medical and first aid assistance from emergency responders and other health care professionals, or to have the patient expeditiously sent to a medical site.

The nature of the emergency is an important factor in guiding practitioner actions. A stroke or heart attack is an emergency situation that warrants immediate action and calling 911, since acupuncture should not be the sole form of treatment. There are situations that acupuncture or other treatments may aid or resuscitate a patient while waiting for first responders to arrive.

As a Replacement for Surgical Procedures Acupuncture may assist patients in the preparation for and recovery from surgery, but should not be used to replace a required surgical procedure.

Malignant Tumours Acupuncture should not be used alone to treat malignant tumours. Needling at the site of the tumour should only be performed by an experienced TCM practitioner who has advanced training. Acupuncture may be used as a complementary measure for the relief of pain or other symptoms, to alleviate side-effects of chemotherapy and radiotherapy, and to improve the quality of life.

  • Specific Areas of the Body Specific areas of the body should not be punctured:
  • fontanelle (or fontanel) area of an infant’s skull
  • external genitalia
  • nipples
  • eyeball

Patients with Bleeding Disorders Needling should be applied with great caution or avoided on patients with bleeding and clotting disorders. These cautions extend to patients who are on anticoagulant therapy or taking medications with an anticoagulant effect (with the exception of aspirin).

Broken or Infected Skin Additional precautions need to be considered if needles are required to be inserted through sites that are inflamed, irritated, or have skin lesions or broken skin.

Deep Puncturing near Vital Organs or Sensitive Areas Points located near large blood vessels or vital organs are contraindicated for deep insertion or should not be punctured.

  • Additional Precautions Acupuncture should not be conducted, or conducted only with additional precautions, on patients who:
  • are intoxicated or under the influence of drugs;
  • have a very empty stomach, fatigued or weak because of possible reactions to treatment;
  • have just completed vigorous physical activity;
  • are suffering uncontrolled movements;
  • are confused, emotionally unstable, non-cooperative, or have a needle phobia;
  • are unstable diabetics for which extra care is required because of possible effects on blood sugar levels and blood circulation at the extremities (peripheral circulation);
  • are on steroids that may impede healing;
  • have a pacemaker or other electronic implants (if using electro- acupuncture).
  • Certain patients are at greater than normal risk for infection from acupuncture: these include patients with:
  • recent heart valve surgery;
  • diabetes;
  • myocardial damage from previous infection of the heart muscle;
  • suppressed immune systems from previous infections, or from use of anti- rejection medications or high-doses of steroids.

Reference the Table of Points: CTCMA-BC Dangerous/Cautionary Points (CTCMA-BC) in Appendix 1 for a more complete examination of precautions.

在從業人員具備技術與訓練的前提下，許多針灸穴位對病人幾乎或完全沒有固有風險。然而，有些穴位帶有特殊的潛在風險；若從業人員缺乏特定的經驗與訓練，對病人造成嚴重傷害的可能性便會提高。

從業人員選穴可依其知識、經驗與技術而調整。對某些從業人員而言，選穴應審慎地加以限制。隨著經驗與技術的增長，可運用的穴位與技術範圍或可擴大，但仍有一些穴位與手法，除非在極為特殊的情況下由技術精湛的專業人員施行，否則不應為之。

由於針灸的詮釋與監管要求各異，要訂立絕對禁忌實屬不易。基於提供安全且專業的病人照護，針灸在下列情況應極為謹慎地施行或避免。

懷孕期間。孕期施行針灸應極為謹慎。上腹部與腰骶部的穴位，以及會引起強烈感應的穴位，應極其小心地針刺，且僅可由經驗豐富、技術純熟的從業人員施行。某些耳穴亦可能引產。有關孕期針灸的更詳細指引，請參照附錄一與可靠著作。

醫療緊急狀況。在緊急情況下，從業人員應於其界定的執業範圍內運用健全判斷。醫療緊急狀況可能需要受過訓練的急救或 CPR 技術。其他情況可能需要從業人員終止治療，向緊急救護人員及其他醫療專業人員尋求醫療與急救協助，或迅速將病人送往醫療處所。緊急狀況的性質是指引從業人員作為的重要因素。中風或心臟病發作屬緊急狀況，須立即行動並撥打 911，因針灸不應作為唯一的治療方式。在某些情況下，針灸或其他治療或可在等待第一線救護人員抵達時，協助或搶救病人。

作為外科手術的替代。針灸可協助病人為手術做準備並從中康復，但不應用來取代必要的外科手術。

惡性腫瘤。不應單獨以針灸治療惡性腫瘤。在腫瘤部位針刺，僅可由受過進階訓練、經驗豐富的中醫從業人員施行。針灸可作為輔助措施，以緩解疼痛或其他症狀、減輕化療與放療的副作用，並改善生活品質。

身體特定部位。身體特定部位不應針刺：嬰兒顱骨的囟門部位；外生殖器；乳頭；眼球。

有出血性疾病的病人。對有出血與凝血障礙的病人，針刺應極為謹慎或避免。此等注意事項亦延伸至正在接受抗凝血治療，或服用具抗凝血作用藥物的病人（阿斯匹靈除外）。

破損或感染的皮膚。若需經由發炎、受刺激，或有皮膚病灶或皮膚破損的部位進針，須考量採取額外的防護措施。

在重要器官或敏感部位附近深刺。位於大血管或重要器官附近的穴位，禁忌深刺，或不應針刺。

其他注意事項。對於下列病人，不應施行針灸，或僅在採取額外防護措施下施行：

  • 酒醉或受藥物影響者；
  • 因空腹、疲勞或虛弱而可能對治療產生反應者；
  • 剛完成劇烈體能活動者；
  • 有無法控制之肢體動作者；
  • 意識混亂、情緒不穩、不配合，或有暈針恐懼（畏針）者；
  • 血糖不穩定的糖尿病患，因可能影響血糖值與四肢末梢循環，需格外小心；
  • 正在服用可能妨礙癒合之類固醇者；
  • 裝有心律調節器或其他電子植入物者（若使用電針）。

某些病人因針灸而受感染的風險高於一般人，包括：近期接受心臟瓣膜手術者；糖尿病患；曾因心肌感染而致心肌受損者；因過往感染，或因使用抗排斥藥物或高劑量類固醇而免疫系統受抑制者。

有關注意事項更完整的探討，請參照附錄一「穴位表：CTCMA-BC 危險／慎用穴位」。

4.8 Managing Adverse Reactions to Acupuncture Treatment 針灸治療不良反應的處理

Acupuncture is a safe procedure when conducted by trained and skilled practitioners who have taken informed and thoughtful precautions and are prepared to respond to accidents or adverse reactions of patients in their care.

  • Practitioners are expected to identify symptoms, causes and management strategies related to the adverse reactions and accidents such as:
  • fainting
  • bent needle
  • stuck needle
  • broken needle

The following sections provide summary information related to these adverse reactions as required for the course but is not intended to be comprehensive. Practitioners are expected to continually expand their professional learning to effectively identify and manage adverse reactions or side effects to acupuncture treatment.

Terminology Side Effects are undesirable effects which occurs in addition to the desired therapeutic effect of treatment. Side effects are unwanted, but are often unavoidable because they are predictable reactions to the treatment in many patients.

Adverse reactions are unexpected and undesirable effects that were not predicted or foreseen. The reasons for adverse reaction vary greatly based on the current mental, emotional, and physical status of the patient.

4.8.1 Fainting A patient may feel faint during acupuncture treatment. Especially for patients that have not had previous treatments, patients should be informed of this possibility and treatment should be done gently while the patient is lying down.

Fainting can be the result of the patient being nervous, weak, fatigued, or overly hungry. It may also be the result of uncomfortable positioning, overly forceful needle manipulation, or over stimulation.

  • Patients should be monitored for symptoms that may indicate that a fainting spell may be approaching. Symptoms during treatment of potential fainting include:
  • feeling ill, nausea, possible vomiting
  • dizziness, vertigo, giddiness
  • seeing movements or swaying of nearby objects
  • oppressive sensation in the chest, palpitations
  • complexion or lips turn pale

More severe cases may be shown by weakened pulse, cold hands or feet, cold sweating, lowered blood pressure or loss of consciousness.

If the practitioner identifies symptoms of pending fainting, carefully remove needles and ensure the patient is lying flat on their back with head down and feet elevated. The patient should be offered sips of warm water or water with sugar. In most instances, patients will recover following rest, but if symptoms persist, seek medical assistance.

The practitioner can reduce the likelihood of fainting by attending to gentle needle manipulation, and monitoring early warning signs (such as changes to complexion colour and signs of disorientation). Patients that may be predisposed to fainting should be treated while lying down or firmly supported while sitting up for treatment.

4.8.2 Stuck Needle Following insertion of the needle, it may be difficult to rotate, lift and thrust, or withdraw. The cause of a stuck needle is often the result of a patient muscle spasm or sudden movement, but can also be the result of rotation with too wide an amplitude or rotating in only one direction causing the needle shaft to tangle with muscle fibres.

The key to resolving a stuck needle is to reassure the patient and ask him or her to relax. It may be necessary to leave the needle in for a while to allow for relaxation, or to tap or massage around the point. It is often helpful to needle a nearby point to help relax the muscle. If needles are still entangled in fibrous tissue, needles can be slightly and gently rotated in the opposite direction to loosen and allow for withdrawal.

  • 4.8.3 Broken or Bent Needle There are many factors that may cause a needle to break or bend, including:
  • poor needle quality
  • erosion or cracks between the shaft and the handle
  • sudden movement or strong muscle spasm by the patient
  • improper withdrawal of a stuck needle or bent needle
  • excessive force manipulating the needle
  • needle is struck by external force (e.g., practitioner extends to reach another point and accidentally leans against an existing insertion)

A needle that is bent during insertion should be withdrawn and replaced by another. Excessive force should not be used when manipulating needles, especially when lifting and thrusting. The most common part of the needle that is prone to breaking is at the junction of the handle and the shaft. For this reason, it is strongly recommended that needles never be inserted up to the handle, and at least one-quarter (1/4) of the shaft is always being kept above the skin. Risks of broken needles can be further reduced by carefully examining needles prior to treatment, and not using excessive force to manipulate needles. It is recommended to always remind patients to remain still during acupuncture treatment.

If a needle breaks, ask the patient to stay still and calm so that any movements do not cause the broken part of the needle to sink deeper into the tissues.

If a portion of the broken needle can be seen above the skin, remove it gently with forceps.

If the needle is at skin level, gently press around the site until the broken end is exposed, and then remove it with forceps. If the broken needle shaft is completely under the skin, seek medical assistance. Do not cut the skin to allow access to the needle. Surgical intervention may be required.

Properly Positioning the Patient Practitioners must be aware that the proper positioning of patients can reduce the likelihood of fainting or of causing sudden movements that can result in bent or stuck needles. Depending on the area to be needled, the practitioner should carefully consider both the comfort of the patient as well as the requirements for effective and safe insertion and manipulation of the needle. Both the patient and the practitioner should feel comfortable.

若由受過訓練、技術純熟的從業人員施行，且其已採取審慎而周全的防護措施，並準備好因應所照護病人的意外或不良反應，針灸即是一項安全的程序。

從業人員應能辨識與下列不良反應及意外相關的症狀、成因與處理策略：暈針；彎針；滯針（滯針）；斷針。

以下各節提供本課程所需之這些不良反應的摘要資訊，但無意求全。從業人員應持續拓展其專業學習，以有效辨識並處理不良反應或副作用。

名詞說明。副作用是指伴隨治療所欲達成之療效而出現的非期望效應。副作用雖不受歡迎，但往往難以避免，因其是許多病人對治療可預期的反應。不良反應是指未曾預測或預見、非預期且非期望的效應。不良反應的成因因病人當下的心理、情緒與身體狀態而大不相同。

4.8.1 暈針。病人在針灸治療中可能感到頭暈。尤其是未曾接受過治療的病人，應告知其此一可能性，並在病人躺臥時輕柔地施治。

暈針可能因病人緊張、虛弱、疲勞或過度飢餓所致，也可能因姿勢不適、行針過於用力或刺激過度而引起。治療中可能暈針的症狀包括：感到不適、噁心、可能嘔吐；頭暈、眩暈；看到鄰近物體晃動或搖擺；胸悶、心悸；面色或嘴唇轉白。較嚴重者可能出現脈弱、手腳冰冷、冒冷汗、血壓下降或意識喪失。

若從業人員發現即將暈針的症狀，應小心出針，並確保病人平躺、頭低腳高。應讓病人啜飲溫水或糖水。多數情況下，病人經休息後會恢復；但若症狀持續，應尋求醫療協助。從業人員可藉由留意輕柔行針並觀察早期警訊，降低暈針的可能性。易於暈針的病人，應在躺臥或坐姿中獲得穩固支撐的情況下施治。

4.8.2 滯針。進針後，可能難以捻轉、提插或出針。滯針的成因常為病人肌肉痙攣或突然移動，但也可能因捻轉幅度過大，或僅朝單一方向捻轉，導致針身與肌纖維纏繞所致。

解決滯針的關鍵在於安撫病人，請其放鬆。或有必要將針留置片刻以待放鬆，或於穴位周圍輕拍或按摩。針刺鄰近穴位以幫助肌肉放鬆，往往有所助益。若針仍纏於纖維組織中，可將針朝相反方向輕微、緩慢地捻轉，以鬆解並利於出針。

4.8.3 斷針或彎針。導致針斷裂或彎曲的因素很多，包括：針具品質不佳；針身與針柄接合處的鏽蝕或裂痕；病人突然移動或劇烈肌肉痙攣；不當地拔出滯針或彎針；行針用力過度；針受外力撞擊（如從業人員伸手去取另一穴位時，不慎倚壓到已刺入的針）。

進針時彎曲的針應予拔出並更換另一支。行針時不應用力過度，尤其是提插時。針最常斷裂的部位是針柄與針身的接合處。因此，強烈建議切勿將針刺至針柄處，並始終保留至少四分之一（1/4）的針身露於皮膚之上。治療前仔細檢查針具、且不過度用力，可進一步降低斷針風險。建議務必提醒病人於針灸治療中保持不動。

若針斷裂，請病人保持不動且鎮定，以免任何移動使斷針部分陷入組織更深處。若斷針有一段可見於皮膚之上，以鉗子輕輕取出。若針斷在皮膚表面高度，輕輕按壓該部位周圍，直到斷端露出，再以鉗子取出。若斷針身完全沒入皮下，應尋求醫療協助。切勿切開皮膚以取針，可能需要外科介入。

妥善安置病人姿勢。從業人員必須知道，妥善安置病人姿勢，可降低暈針的可能性，或避免引起可能導致彎針或滯針的突然移動。從業人員應依欲針刺的部位，審慎兼顧病人的舒適與有效、安全進針及行針的需求。病人與從業人員雙方都應感到舒適。

4.9 Injury to Vital Organs 重要器官損傷

Accidents may occur near vital organs or very sensitive areas as a result of the site chosen, the needle depth, the needle direction or angle, the manipulation technique used, the stimulation provided, and other factors. Injuries can be avoided if additional precautions are taken by the practitioner when treating points near vital organs.

Accidents must be managed effectively but any injury to vital organs may be serious and may require the practitioner to act urgently to arrange medical assistance.

  • Lung and Pleura
  • Attend very carefully to the depth and angle of inserted needles on the chest, back, or immediately above the clavicle (supraclavicular fossa).
  • Deep insertion may cause collapsed lung (traumatic pneumothorax). Attend to symptoms such as cough, chest pain, and difficult or laboured breath (dyspnoea) that happen immediately or gradually develop in the next few hours following treatment.
  • Pneumothorax is one of the most frequently reported complications (point Jianjing GB 21 is the most commonly reported point).
  • Points on the Chest, Back and Abdomen
  • Attend carefully to the depth and direction of inserted needles.
  • Liver, Spleen and Kidney
  • Attend carefully to the depth and direction of inserted needles.
  • A liver or spleen puncture may cause pain or tenderness that is localized, a tear with associated bleeding, or stiffness of the abdominal muscles.
  • A kidney puncture may cause pain in the lumbar region and the patient may see evidence of blood in the urine (haematuria). If bleeding continues, the patient may suffer shock due to dropping blood pressure.
  • Circulatory System
  • Attend carefully to the depth and direction of inserted needles. Feel for the pulse to locate artery.
  • Bleeding caused by penetration of shallow blood vessels can often be stopped by applying direct pressure.
  • Additional precautions should be taken in needling areas of poor circulation where there is a risk of infection (e.g. varicose veins).
  • Avoid puncturing of arteries.
  • Central Nervous System
  • Exercise caution at points between or beside the upper cervical vertebrae (Yamen GV 15, Fengfu GV 16). Needling the lower half of the brainstem (medulla oblongata) may cause headaches, nausea, vomiting, and slowed respiration. This can be followed by convulsions, paralysis or coma. Seek immediate medical assistance.
  • Exercise extreme caution near the spinal cord. Deep insertions may cause lightening pain for the patient or more serious complications such as loss of sensation or movement.
  • Other Points Other points that require significant precautions and specialized training include:
  • near the carotid artery (Renying ST 9)
  • near the femoral artery (Jimen SP 11, Chongmen SP 12)
  • on the radial artery (Taiyuan LU 9, Jingqu LU 8)
  • points near the eyes (Jingming BL 1, Chengqi ST 1)
  • front of the trachea (Tiantu CV 22)

因所選部位、針刺深度、進針方向或角度、所用行針手法、所施刺激及其他因素，意外可能發生於重要器官或極敏感部位附近。從業人員在治療重要器官附近的穴位時，若採取額外防護措施，即可避免傷害。意外必須妥善處理，但任何重要器官的損傷都可能嚴重，可能需要從業人員緊急安排醫療協助。

肺與胸膜

  • 在胸部、背部，或緊鄰鎖骨上方（鎖骨上窩）針刺時，須極為謹慎地留意進針深度與角度。
  • 深刺可能造成肺塌陷（外傷性氣胸）。留意咳嗽、胸痛，以及呼吸困難（呼吸急促）等於治療後立即出現，或於其後數小時內逐漸發展的症狀。
  • 氣胸是最常被通報的併發症之一（肩井穴 GB 21 是最常被通報的穴位）。

胸、背與腹部穴位——須謹慎留意進針的深度與方向。

肝、脾與腎

  • 謹慎留意進針的深度與方向。
  • 刺傷肝或脾可能造成局部疼痛或壓痛、伴隨出血的撕裂傷，或腹肌僵硬。
  • 刺傷腎可能造成腰部疼痛，病人可能出現血尿。若持續出血，病人可能因血壓下降而休克。

循環系統

  • 謹慎留意進針的深度與方向。以觸摸脈搏來定位動脈。
  • 刺穿淺層血管所致的出血，通常可藉直接加壓止血。
  • 在循環不良且有感染風險的部位（如靜脈曲張）針刺時，應採取額外防護措施。
  • 避免刺穿動脈。

中樞神經系統

  • 在上頸椎之間或旁側的穴位（啞門 GV 15、風府 GV 16）須謹慎。針刺腦幹下半部（延髓）可能造成頭痛、噁心、嘔吐與呼吸變慢，其後可能出現抽搐、癱瘓或昏迷。應立即尋求醫療協助。
  • 在脊髓附近須極為謹慎。深刺可能使病人產生閃電般的疼痛，或造成如喪失感覺或運動能力等更嚴重的併發症。

其他須高度防護與專門訓練的穴位包括：頸動脈附近（人迎 ST 9）；股動脈附近（箕門 SP 11、衝門 SP 12）；橈動脈上（太淵 LU 9、經渠 LU 8）；眼周穴位（睛明 BL 1、承泣 ST 1）；氣管前方（天突 CV 22）。

4.10 Other Possible Side Effects and Adverse Reactions 其他可能的副作用與不良反應

The following side effects and adverse reactions can be experienced by patients as a result of treatment.

  • Acupuncture
  • De-Qi (acupuncture sensation) - a commonly experienced sensation of warmth, tingling or tightness. (Note: Other sensations include numbness, mild electrical shooting sensation that last for seconds, distension, soreness, pulsation like waves. These are all good sensations (or good pain) of de qi. There is also sharp, excruciating and uncomfortable pain (or bad pain) that can be experienced. Practitioners should stop the insertion or manipulation and slightly withdraw the needle to relieve the bad pain.)
  • common reactions are feelings of light-headedness, or slight disorientation or euphoria
  • feelings of cold if needles are retained for longer periods
  • minor bleeding or bruising due to penetration of small blood vessels
  • minor swelling or bruising from hematoma
  • fatigue caused by the temporary lowering of blood pressure (may also cause fainting) (Note: It is common for patient to feel fatigued or relaxed during or after treatment. It is highly recommended that the patient experience no intensive stimulation for the rest of the day. Going to sleep early is also recommended after acupuncture treatments.)
  • dizziness, light-headedness, vertigo may occur if too many needles are used or if the patient has a weak constitution (Note: caution should be taken and needles should be immediately removed if symptoms do not subside.)
  • rashes, itching, discomfort at site of insertion caused by allergic reactions to metals against their skin
  • Moxibustion
  • blisters and 2nd degree burns if burning herbs are too close to the skin or left too long.
  • Cupping and gua sha
  • the suction action of cupping, to reduce the stagnation of qi and blood, draws blood close to the skin surface and may cause mild bruising, redness and some local tenderness for several days. Patients are advised to stay warm and avoid chills for 1 or 2 days.
  • like cupping, the scraping action of gua sha to stimulate circulation of qi and blood may cause mild bruising, redness and some local tenderness for several days. Patients are advised to stay warm and avoid chills for 1 or 2 days.
  • Tapping, Plum Blossom, Bleeding, Pricking
  • bruising or bleeding caused by the intentional drawing of blood, or using multiple needles that may rupture small blood vessels.
  • Electrical Stimulation (PENS, TENS)
  • temporary tingling due to electrical stimulation.
  • electro-stimulator, if not used properly (on for too long, setting is too high) or poorly maintained, can cause nerve damage.
  • Tuina
  • Tuina therapy may cause soreness – Tuina and other manual therapies often make muscles and tendons sore from various techniques such as pressing, stretching, and tapping on the meridians system. De-Qi sensation may be more pronounced.
  • Tuina therapy may cause bruising in patients who are prone to bruising.
  • Blood-letting
  • slight bruising may occur at the site where the lancet is inserted.

治療後，病人可能出現下列副作用與不良反應。

針灸

  • 得氣（針感）——常見的溫熱、麻刺或緊繃感。（註：其他感受包括麻木、持續數秒的輕微電竄感、脹、痠、如波浪般的搏動。這些都是得氣的良好感受（或稱「好痛」）。也可能出現尖銳、劇烈而不適的疼痛（或稱「壞痛」）。從業人員應停止進針或行針，並略微退針，以緩解此種壞痛。）
  • 常見反應為頭暈感，或輕微的迷失方向感或欣快感；
  • 留針較久時可能感到寒冷；
  • 因刺穿小血管而輕微出血或瘀青；
  • 因血腫而輕微腫脹或瘀青；
  • 因血壓暫時下降所致的疲倦（亦可能引起暈針）。（註：病人於治療中或治療後感到疲倦或放鬆很常見。高度建議病人當日剩餘時間避免劇烈刺激。針灸治療後亦建議早點就寢。）
  • 若使用針數過多，或病人體質虛弱，可能出現頭暈、暈眩、眩暈。（註：應謹慎，若症狀未緩解應立即出針。）
  • 因皮膚對金屬的過敏反應，於進針處出現皮疹、搔癢、不適。

艾灸——若燃燒的藥材過於貼近皮膚或留置過久，可能造成水泡與二度燒傷。

拔罐與刮痧——拔罐藉吸力以疏通氣血瘀滯，會將血液引至皮膚表面附近，可能造成數日的輕微瘀青、發紅與部分局部壓痛。建議病人在 1 至 2 天內保暖並避免受寒。與拔罐相同，刮痧的刮拭動作也可能造成數日的輕微瘀青、發紅與部分局部壓痛。

叩刺、梅花針、放血、點刺——因刻意引血，或使用多針而可能弄破小血管，造成瘀青或出血。

電刺激（PENS、TENS）——因電刺激而暫時麻刺。電刺激器若使用不當（開啟過久、設定過高）或維護不良，可能造成神經損傷。

推拿——可能造成痠痛，因推拿及其他徒手療法常以按壓、伸展、叩擊經絡系統等手法，使肌肉與肌腱痠痛。得氣感可能較為明顯。對容易瘀青的病人，推拿可能造成瘀青。

放血——採血針刺入處可能出現輕微瘀青。

4.11 Specific Considerations Related to Other Techniques 與其他技術相關的具體考量

All ancillary techniques and therapies must be conducted in a safe manner, and practitioners must be properly trained in their use.

  • 4.11.1 Electrical Stimulation Contraindications to Electrical Stimulations
  • Do not use with patients with pacemakers or other electronic implants.
  • Do not use with patients with a history of seizures.
  • Due to risks of interference with the heart muscle, two branches of the same electrode should always be on the same side of the patient's body. The circuit should never cross from one side of the back or the chest to the other side (i.e., current does not cross the midsagittal or median plane).
  • Do not apply stimulation in regions close to the heart.
  • Precautions and Considerations
  • Carefully examine the equipment before each treatment. Clean and disinfect required surfaces prior to treatment.
  • Ensure that all dials are set to zero before applying current or attaching the clips to the needles.
  • Increase intensity gradually.
  • Throughout the treatment, make sure that the patient is not in discomfort. Stimulation should never become painful.
  • Carefully monitor the patient to prevent neural injury.
  • Carefully consider the time requirements of treatment.
  • All dials must be set to zero before switching off power and removing connections.
  • Use additional precautions with older patients or with frail patients.
  • Ensure that all electrical stimulators, acupuncture scope, probes and hand held devices, if using 110 voltages, are plugged into a surge protection bar, not directly to the outlet. The consequence in a power surge could transfer extra current to the patient. For portability, many of these electrical devices provide optional battery back-up. Inspection of medical devices on a regular basis is important to ensure effective treatment and safety of the patient.

4.11.2 Moxibustion Accidents may occur using moxibustion. Practitioners must be fully trained and competent in the procedure and must consider the precautions/considerations.

The patient should never be left unattended at any point during a procedure involving the use of any form of moxibustion.

See also Appendix 1 Table of Points CTCMA- BC Dangerous Cautionary Points.

  • Precautions and Considerations
  • Be cautious when using the treatment for pregnant patient.
  • Be cautious using the treatment near mucous membranes.
  • Be cautious of using the treatment on hypo-sensitive or hyper-sensitive patients.
  • Be cautious of using the treatment over main arteries or organs and on the face or sensitive areas (due to increased vulnerability to burns).
  • The use of scarring moxibustion should be evaluated carefully to determine if it is a required method for treatment as it causes scarring.
  • Use with extreme caution on patients with decreased sensitivity to heat.
  • Needling after moxibustion requires the skin to be swabbed.
  • Moxibustion is a fire hazard. Practitioners need increased awareness about the use of fire extinguishers and emergency procedures in the clinic.
  • Practitioners using moxibustion (particularly direct moxibustion) are encouraged to be very clear and explicit with patients. Ensure that signed consent to treatment forms identify possible risks of the procedure.
  • Heated needle methods require increased attention to risk management procedures. Use a disposable heat shield under the needle and when burnt moxa is being removed from the needle.
  • Managing Burns
  • Moxibustion carries a risk of burns which requires practitioners to carefully attend to risk management procedures and legal actions as a result of burns and scars.
  • Small blisters should be protected and may be allowed to heal without the need of puncturing.
  • Severe burns or large blister may need to be seen by a physician.
  • A large blister may need to be treated as follows: o Wash hands thoroughly. o Disinfect the blister area. o Puncture the blister with a sterilized needle and drain hygienically. o Apply an appropriate dressing of sterile gauze. o See a physician as required.

As appropriate, it is highly recommended that treatment rooms have a wireless call button or alert button to notify the practitioner or staff of any situations that require swift response (e.g., strong electro-acupuncture stimulation, heat lamp too strong, patient feeling discomfort or in an uncomfortable position, patient needing to use the washroom during the treatment). The call button also assures more sensitive or anxious patients that they can call the practitioner without the embarrassment of having to call

out loudly. If a call button is not used, the practitioner should stay in the treatment room or regularly check on the patient at frequent intervals.

4.11.3 Heat Lamps For patient safety and carefully managed risk, the use of heat lamps requires the practitioner to cautiously attend to the operation and maintenance of the equipment. Heat lamps must be used and maintained in strict accordance to manufacturer’s instructions. It is essential that practitioner frequently and regularly monitor the heat lamps to confirm and ensure that the lamps are operating as intended and maintained in safe working order.

所有輔助技術與療法都必須以安全的方式施行，從業人員在使用上必須受過適當訓練。

4.11.1 電刺激

禁忌：

  • 裝有心律調節器或其他電子植入物的病人不可使用。
  • 有癲癇發作病史的病人不可使用。
  • 由於有干擾心肌的風險，同一電極的兩個分支應始終位於病人身體的同一側。電路絕不可從背部或胸部的一側跨越至另一側（即電流不跨越正中矢狀面或正中面）。
  • 勿在鄰近心臟的部位施加刺激。

注意事項與考量：

  • 每次治療前仔細檢查設備。治療前清潔並消毒所需表面。
  • 在通電或將夾具夾至針上之前，確保所有旋鈕歸零。
  • 逐漸增強強度。
  • 整個治療過程中，確保病人無不適。刺激絕不應變得疼痛。
  • 謹慎監看病人，以防神經損傷。
  • 謹慎考量治療的時間需求。
  • 關閉電源、移除連接之前，所有旋鈕都必須歸零。
  • 對年長或體弱的病人採取額外防護措施。
  • 確保所有電刺激器、針灸鏡、探頭與手持裝置，若使用 110 伏特電壓，都插在突波保護插座上，而非直接插入牆上插座。電源突波可能將額外電流傳給病人。許多裝置提供選配的電池備援。定期檢查醫療器材，對有效治療與病人安全十分重要。

4.11.2 艾灸
使用艾灸可能發生意外。從業人員必須在此程序上受過完整訓練並能勝任，且必須考量各項注意事項。在涉及任何形式艾灸的過程中，任何時刻都不應讓病人無人看顧。另見附錄一。

注意事項與考量：

  • 對孕婦施行此治療時應謹慎。
  • 在黏膜附近施行此治療時應謹慎。
  • 對感覺遲鈍或感覺過敏的病人施行此治療時應謹慎。
  • 在大動脈或器官上方，以及臉部或敏感部位施行此治療時應謹慎（因較易灼傷）。
  • 由於瘢痕灸會造成瘢痕，是否採用應審慎評估，判斷其是否為所需的方法。
  • 對熱感覺降低的病人使用時應極為謹慎。
  • 艾灸後針刺，須先擦拭消毒皮膚。
  • 艾灸具火災危險。從業人員需提高對診所內滅火器使用與緊急程序的警覺。
  • 鼓勵使用艾灸（尤其是直接灸）的從業人員，向病人清楚明確地說明。確保簽署的同意書載明可能的風險。
  • 溫針法需更留意風險管理。在針下方，以及清除燒過的艾絨時，使用拋棄式隔熱片。

灼傷的處理：

  • 艾灸帶有灼傷的風險，這要求從業人員審慎留意風險管理程序，以及因灼傷與瘢痕而可能衍生的法律行動。
  • 小水泡應加以保護，可讓其自行癒合，無須刺破。
  • 嚴重灼傷或大水泡可能須由醫師診治。
  • 大水泡可能須依下列方式處理：徹底洗手；消毒水泡部位；以滅菌針刺破水泡並衛生地引流；敷以適當的無菌紗布；視需要就醫。

視情況，高度建議治療室設有無線呼叫鈕或警示鈕，以便就需迅速回應的狀況通知從業人員或員工（如電針刺激過強、烤燈過強、病人不適、病人於治療中需如廁）。呼叫鈕也讓較敏感或焦慮的病人安心，知道自己可呼叫從業人員而不必尷尬地大聲呼喊。若未設呼叫鈕，從業人員應留在治療室內，或頻繁地定時查看病人。

4.11.3 烤燈
為了病人安全與審慎的風險管理，使用烤燈要求從業人員謹慎留意設備的操作與維護。烤燈必須嚴格依照製造商指示使用與維護。從業人員頻繁且定期監看烤燈，確認其依預期運作並維持在安全的運作狀態，至關重要。

4.12 Acupuncture Treatment in Unfamiliar Settings 在陌生環境中的針灸治療

  • Practitioners that make home visits are expected to meet the health and safety standards that are equivalent to working in a clinic.
  • The clinic, home, or business premises of the practitioner must have the facilities to establish a clean and sterile setting for the establishment of a travel kit. The practitioner will require at least one room where equipment can be adequately disinfected and sterilized (as required), instruments and materials that will be required can be stored in a clean environment; and clinical waste, used sharps, and soiled equipment and linens can be stored temporarily but safely.
  • The setting for the off-site treatment (e.g., a patient’s home) should allow for safe treatment. The room should have adequate lighting, be clean, and have easy access to sanitary hand washing facilities.
  • It must not be assumed that any needed clean supplies are available on site. Pack the travel pack with all required supplies and equipment required for treatment. Include supplies to allow for sanitary hand washing, creating sanitary work and treatment surfaces, and to establish a clean field. (See Travel Kit Contents.)
  • Following treatment, all used sharps must be disposed of in a portable sharps container, all clinical waste appropriately collected for removal and disposal, and all other waste must be disposed of consistent with local requirements.
  • Allow adequate time in the schedule of visitations to confirm that the patient is not suffering adverse reactions before leaving.

If you carry out treatment at a patient's home, you must ensure that, as much as possible, the treatment is carried out in a clean room with sufficient lighting and convenient access to hand washing facilities. As well as required instruments and materials, the practitioner should bring supplies to ensure appropriate hand washing (liquid soap, sanitizer, paper towels, etc.) and to establish a clean field.

Travel Kit Contents Sterile equipment and materials can be placed in an easily disinfected portable container that can be sealed for easy, sanitary transport.

  • The following sterile items can be carried in this container:
  • single-use disposable needles in a separate sealed plastic bag
  • supplies to establish a clean field, each in separate sealed plastic bags (e.g., sterile gauze, cotton ball, sterile linen or equivalent). Use sterile tongs to aid packaging
  • disposable medical gloves as required
               •   sterile, packaged equipment such as tweezers or forceps (package should indicate
                   date of sterilization)
               •   package 70% isopropyl alcohol swabs (or equivalent) in a separate container.
  • Packaged separately from sterile items:
  • a plastic lined bag to receive clinical waste
  • portable sharps container appropriately labelled.
  • Additional Items
  • liquid soap for hand washing, hand sanitizer, sealed and clean paper towels or equivalent (paper towels should transferred immediately in aplastic sealed bag to keep clean)
  • clean paper rolls (or equivalent) to cover work surfaces or patient’s bed or couch where treatment will be conducted
  • required documents (e.g., consent to treatment forms), record keeping notes to document consultation.
  • Preparing the Clean Field
  • Wash your hands.
  • Select the area for the clean field and clean and dry the area thoroughly.
  • Wash your hands.
  • Cover the chosen area with your clean field linen or equivalent.
  • Arrange materials and instruments on the clean field. Open the storage bags and remove items in such a way as to minimize handling.
  • The sharps container and waste bags are placed away from the clean field.

Safe Insertion and Removal of Needle The hygienic safe insertion and removal of the needle should follow the same procedures (aseptic technique) as outlined for on-site needling (see Section 4.4).

進行到府出診的從業人員，應達到與在診所工作相當的健康與安全標準。

  • 從業人員的診所、住家或營業場所，必須具備設施，以建立潔淨、無菌的環境來準備出診包。從業人員至少需要一個房間，能充分消毒與滅菌設備、在潔淨環境中存放器械與材料，並能暫時但安全地存放臨床廢棄物、用過的銳器，以及髒污的設備與布巾。
  • 外出治療的場所（如病人住家）應能容許安全治療。房間應有充足照明、保持潔淨，並易於使用衛生的洗手設施。
  • 不可假設現場備有任何所需的潔淨用品。出診包應打包好所有必需的用品與設備，包括可供衛生洗手、建立衛生工作與治療表面，以及建立清潔區的用品。
  • 治療後，所有用過的銳器都必須丟入攜帶式銳器容器，所有臨床廢棄物適當收集以便清運與處置，其餘所有廢棄物則依當地要求處置。
  • 在出診行程中預留充足時間，於離開前確認病人未出現不良反應。

若您在病人住家進行治療，必須盡可能確保治療於潔淨、照明充足且便於使用洗手設施的房間中進行。除必需的器械與材料外，從業人員還應攜帶用品，以確保適當洗手（液體肥皂、乾洗手液、紙巾等）並建立清潔區。

出診包內容。無菌設備與材料可置於易於消毒、可密封以便衛生搬運的攜帶式容器中。此容器可攜帶下列無菌物品：

  • 裝於另一密封塑膠袋中的一次性拋棄式針具；
  • 建立清潔區的用品，各自裝於獨立的密封塑膠袋中（如無菌紗布、棉球、無菌布巾或同等物）。以無菌鉗協助包裝；
  • 視需要的拋棄式醫療手套；
  • 無菌且包裝好的器械，如鑷子或鉗子（包裝上應標明滅菌日期）；
  • 另裝於獨立容器中、包裝好的 70% 異丙醇棉片（或同等物）。

與無菌物品分開包裝者：接收臨床廢棄物、內襯塑膠的袋子；妥善標示的攜帶式銳器容器。

其他物品：洗手用的液體肥皂、乾洗手液、密封潔淨的紙巾或同等物；覆蓋工作表面或病人床、沙發用的潔淨捲筒紙（或同等物）；必需的文件（如治療同意書）、記錄用的紀錄。

準備清潔區。洗手。選定清潔區的區域，徹底清潔並擦乾。再次洗手。以您的清潔區布巾或同等物覆蓋所選區域。在清潔區上擺放材料與器械，開啟收納袋並以盡量減少觸碰的方式取出物品。銳器容器與廢物袋置於遠離清潔區之處。

安全進針與出針。衛生且安全的進針與出針，應遵循與現場針刺相同的程序（無菌技術）（見第 4.4 節）。

4.13 Patient Privacy and Draping 病人隱私與遮蓋

Practitioners must be in compliance with all regulations related to proper draping of patients, including the applicable College’s Practice Standards. Establishing and maintaining ethical draping procedures is an essential element of risk management for the acupuncturist.

Practitioners have a professional and moral responsibility to ensure that a patient’s dignity and privacy is maintained in the conduct of consented treatments.

For some treatments, draping may not be required. If the patient has loose fitting clothing, it may be possible for the patient to remain fully clothed during treatment by rolling sleeves above the elbow, pant legs to above the knees, and allowing full exposure to the abdomen or back. It can also be suggested that patients bring loose fitting clothes or shorts that they can change into for treatment.

For other procedures, draping may be necessary. It should be explained fully to the patient why draping is necessary as it relates to their personal safety making needling safer. Draping significantly reduces the opportunity for clothing to inadvertently cover

needles or to having needles left in after treatment. The patient must be given the opportunity to consent to undressing and draping. The patient must be provided with adequate and appropriate draping materials. The practitioner must not be present during undressing and the dignity of the patient must be maintained at all times.

The degree of undress must be based on the treatment requirements. It is recommended that if draping is necessary, unless specific procedures cannot be conducted safely and effectively, females remain clothed in a bra (preferably with a hook at the back) and all patients remain clothed in underwear. If full undress is needed, a female’s breasts and a client’s genitalia and gluteal cleft must remain properly draped at all times except for the safe, comfortable and effective application of treatment requirements.

Every effort must be made by the practitioner to ensure the dignity and modesty of the patient is respected and maintained by the use of maximum proper draping. The use of gowns may be more comfortable in addition to site specific draping of private areas involving woman’s breast, genitalia and gluteal cleft of all patients. Patients should be informed to express any concerns whenever he or she is uncomfortable or unsure of any aspect of the treatment. If the patient is comfortable with not being draped or clothed, it is the duty of the practitioner to insist, for the practitioner’s protection, that the patient be properly draped. At all times, the practitioner must establish an uncompromised professional environment of treatment.

The following graphic is a visual guide to draping that is useful for many common situations. The graphic is for reference only and is not meant to be comprehensive for all procedures or point selections.

Reference your College’s Practice Standards for additional details related to draping. DRAPING FOR PATIENTS

從業人員必須遵守所有與適當遮蓋病人相關的法規，包括所適用學院的執業標準。建立並維持合乎倫理的遮蓋程序，是針灸師風險管理不可或缺的一環。

從業人員在專業與道德上有責任，確保在施行經同意的治療過程中，維護病人的尊嚴與隱私。

某些治療可能無須遮蓋。若病人穿著寬鬆衣物，或許可讓病人於治療中維持完全著衣，藉由將袖子捲至肘上、褲管捲至膝上，並使腹部或背部完全露出。也可建議病人自備寬鬆衣物或短褲，以便換上接受治療。

其他程序則可能須遮蓋。應向病人充分說明為何需要遮蓋——這關乎其人身安全，使針刺更為安全。遮蓋可大幅減少衣物無意間覆蓋針具，或治療後針具被遺留的機會。必須給予病人機會同意寬衣與遮蓋，並提供病人充足且適當的遮蓋材料。寬衣過程中從業人員不得在場，且須始終維護病人的尊嚴。

寬衣的程度必須依治療需求而定。建議若需遮蓋，除非特定程序無法安全有效地進行，否則女性仍穿著胸罩（最好是背後有掛鉤者），所有病人皆仍穿著內褲。若需完全寬衣，除為安全、舒適且有效地施行治療所需外，女性的乳房，以及病人的生殖器與臀溝，都必須始終妥善遮蓋。

從業人員必須竭盡所能，藉由最大程度的適當遮蓋，確保病人的尊嚴與端莊受到尊重與維護。除了針對女性乳房，以及所有病人生殖器與臀溝等私密部位進行部位性遮蓋外，使用罩袍可能更為舒適。應告知病人，每當其對治療的任何環節感到不適或不確定時，都可表達任何顧慮。若病人對不遮蓋或不著衣感到自在，為保護從業人員本身，從業人員仍有責任堅持病人應妥善遮蓋。從業人員必須始終建立一個毫不妥協的專業治療環境。

有關遮蓋的其他細節，請參照您所屬學院的執業標準。

本課程的資訊並非一份完整的操作手冊。課程教材僅是與中醫執業中安全施行中藥學相關之重要資訊的選擇性摘要。

從業人員應持續掌握與安全、有效地開立及調劑中藥相關的最新研究與文獻。

Safe Procedures & Processes: Herbology安全操作規程：中藥學

The information in this course is not a comprehensive instruction manual. The course materials are only a selective summary of important information related to the safe practice of herbology within a TCM practice.

It is expected that practitioners will continue to remain informed and current with current research and literature related to the safe and effective prescribing and dispensing of herbal medications.

（本章前言，中文從略；左欄為原文逐字內容。）

  • Index of Materia Medica ( Pin-yin, Chinese and Botanical or Pharmaceutical Name) See Appendix 2.
  • Animal and Plant Ingredients in Traditional Medicine (Environment Canada, 2011) See Appendix 3.
